03Procurement and Vendor SelectionBest value needs a reason, not just a label+

Your goal

Connect a procurement recommendation to published criteria, comparable evidence, and an authorized decision process.

This lesson in the book, page 22 ↗

A Read the situation

Best value needs a reason, not just a label

A fictional office evaluates two service proposals using published maximum scores: technical approach 50, service delivery 30, and price 20. Recorded weighted scores are A: 45, 25, 16, total 86; B: 40, 24, 18, total 82. A costs $120,000 and B costs $110,000. The method permits a documented trade-off, but the draft note only says A is best value. Evaluator Dev and procurement officer Marta must explain the scored differences and the $10,000 premium. A vendor's local address is not a published criterion. The award has not been authorized.

Dev: Technical evaluator / Marta: Procurement officer

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What are the recorded totals?

Answer and explanation

A. A has 86 and B has 82.

Adding the three recorded weighted scores produces totals of eighty-six and eighty-two.

2. What is A's price premium?

Answer and explanation

B. $10,000

A's price of one hundred twenty thousand exceeds B's one hundred ten thousand by ten thousand.

3. Which consideration is not a published criterion?

Answer and explanation

A. The vendor's local address

The brief lists three published factors and expressly excludes local address as a criterion.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

The scores are already weighted. Do not apply the 50/30/20 maxima a second time. The totals are 86 and 82. A higher score is relevant under this method, but the permitted trade-off still needs a reasoned record of the benefits and premium.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
procurement
The process of obtaining goods or services under applicable rules.manage procurement
solicitation
A formal request inviting offers under stated requirements.review the solicitation
offeror
A supplier submitting an offer in response to a solicitation.evaluate the offeror
evaluation factor
A stated dimension used to assess proposals.apply the evaluation factors
subfactor
A defined component of a broader evaluation factor.assess the subfactor
relative importance
The stated relationship in significance among evaluation factors.state relative importance
weighted score
A score already adjusted to reflect the specified scoring importance.verify the weighted scores
scoring matrix
A structured record of scores against criteria.reconcile the scoring matrix
technical approach
The proposed method for delivering the required work.evaluate the technical approach
service-delivery plan
The proposed arrangement for providing the required service.assess the service-delivery plan
price premium
The amount by which one price exceeds another.justify the price premium
best value
The most advantageous result under the applicable selection method.explain the best-value judgment
trade-off
A comparison accepting one disadvantage for a supported benefit.document the trade-off
strength
An assessed favorable feature under an evaluation criterion.substantiate a strength
weakness
An assessed unfavorable feature under an evaluation criterion.document a weakness
deficiency
A material failure to meet a stated requirement under the applicable method.identify a deficiency
past performance
Evidence of how a supplier performed on relevant previous work.assess past performance
evaluation narrative
Written reasoning supporting the recorded assessment.complete the evaluation narrative
source selection
The process of choosing a supplier under the applicable procedure.support source selection
award authority
The person or body empowered to authorize the contract award.confirm award authority
conflict of interest
An interest that may improperly influence, or appear to influence, official judgment.disclose a conflict of interest
recusal
Withdrawal from a matter under the applicable conflict-handling process.arrange recusal
audit trail
Records allowing a decision or transaction to be traced.preserve the audit trail
debriefing
A structured explanation of a procurement outcome under applicable procedures.prepare a debriefing

Best value is a conclusion under a specified selection method, not a synonym for lowest price or local supplier. Link each claimed strength to a published factor and proposal evidence. An evaluation recommendation is not the same as an authorized award.

C Notice the language

Challenge the rationale without reopening the rules

A request to explain an evaluation is not necessarily an accusation of bias. Link the judgment to the announced criteria and the submitted evidence. Do not quietly introduce a new preference after seeing which supplier it would favor.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Ask for the rationale
What does best value mean under this selection method?
Return to the criteria
Let us use the published evaluation factors.
Check the arithmetic
The weighted scores total eighty-six for A and eighty-two for B.
Avoid double weighting
These scores have already been weighted.
Name the price difference
A carries a ten-thousand-dollar premium.
Identify the strength
Which submitted feature supports the technical advantage?
Link evidence to judgment
Cite the proposal evidence beside the relevant criterion.
Explain the trade-off
The note must explain why the assessed benefit justifies the premium.
Reject an added preference
Local address is not a published criterion.
Preserve equal treatment
Apply the same evidence standard to both offers.
Clarify the status
This is an evaluation recommendation, not an award.
Check authority
The designated award authority still needs to decide.
Handle a conflict
Any relevant interest should go through the conflict-review process.
Avoid score-only reasoning
The total does not replace the evaluation narrative.
Keep the record reviewable
A reader should be able to trace each conclusion to its evidence.
Close the revision
Revise the rationale without changing the published method.
6 language and precision notes
Under this method
Limits the conclusion to the actual procurement procedure.
Already weighted
Prevents a second adjustment that would distort the scores.
Supports the advantage
Requests evidence for a comparative judgment.
Justifies the premium
Connects a benefit to the additional cost rather than ignoring price.
Not a published criterion
Identifies an impermissible addition under the stated process.
Recommendation, not an award
Separates the evaluation team's work from final authorization.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which evaluation note is strongest?

Answer and explanation

C. Link A's scored strengths to proposal evidence and explain the $10,000 premium.

The strongest note connects the comparative benefits, evidence, and cost under the published method.

2. What should happen to the existing weighted scores?

Answer and explanation

D. Check their arithmetic without applying the weights again.

The scores are already weighted, so a second weighting would distort the recorded method.

3. Which statement preserves authority?

Answer and explanation

A. The evaluation recommends A; the award decision remains pending.

The brief gives the evaluation team a recommendation role while award authorization remains outstanding.

4. Which challenge is appropriate?

Answer and explanation

B. Which published factor and submitted evidence support that claimed strength?

The challenge tests the rationale within the stated rules instead of changing them to fit a desired result.

Check explanations in the book, page 89 ↗

E Conversations

Best value needs a reason, not just a label

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Dev: Technical evaluator / Marta: Procurement officer

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • Best value
  • award authority
  • audit trail
  • conflict of interest
  • evaluation narrative
  • weighted score
  • trade-off
  • scoring matrix
  • price premium
  • evaluation factor
  1. Marta
    I can check the totals, but I cannot sign this rationale yet. Best value is doing a lot of work in the draft. Show me what sits behind it.
  2. Dev
    The 1 gives A forty-five for technical approach, twenty-five for service delivery, and sixteen for price. B has forty, twenty-four, and eighteen respectively.
  3. Marta
    That gives eighty-six and eighty-two. Are those raw marks that still need the fifty, thirty, and twenty weighting applied?
  4. Dev
    No, each 2 is final for that factor. We should check the addition, but applying the weights again would change the evaluation rather than verify it.
  5. Marta
    A is also ten thousand dollars more expensive. The note cannot simply report the higher total and leave that difference unexplained.
  6. Dev
    Agreed. The 3 is part of the decision. A costs one hundred twenty thousand, while B costs one hundred ten thousand. Our method permits a documented trade-off.
  7. Marta
    Which features support the technical difference? A reader should not have to infer a benefit from the score alone.
  8. Dev
    I need to expand the 4. Each assessed strength should point to the submitted material and the technical factor it addresses, rather than a general impression that A seems better.
  9. Marta
    Someone suggested mentioning that A's office is local. Does the solicitation give us a factor for that?
  10. Dev
    No. It is not an 5, so we should not add it now. The published dimensions are technical approach, service delivery, and price, with the stated relative importance.
  11. Marta
    We also need to show that B's stronger price result was recognized, not quietly removed because the team prefers A.
  12. Dev
    The 6 explanation should do that. It must explain why the evidenced benefits of A are judged worth the premium, if that remains the supported recommendation.
  13. Marta
    Is a four-point lead sufficient by itself? The summary says highest score therefore award, which skips the trade-off we have just discussed.
  14. Dev
    No. 7 depends on this selection method and a defensible record. We should not present our scoring example as a rule for every procurement.
  15. Marta
    Before circulation, check that any interests affecting an evaluator have gone through the proper disclosure process.
  16. Dev
    I will confirm the 8 review status. That belongs in the appropriate record, without turning an unsupported suspicion about a colleague into an allegation in the evaluation note.
  17. Marta
    Once the explanation is complete, who actually authorizes the contract award? The draft sounds as though the evaluation team already has.
  18. Dev
    The designated 9 still needs to decide. I will label the document as our recommendation and remove wording that suggests an award has already been made.
  19. Marta
    Good. Keep the published criteria, the verified totals, the evidence, and the premium visible in the revised note.
  20. Dev
    I will preserve that 10. The revision will explain the recommendation under the existing method, not introduce a new preference to make the result easier to defend.

Answers and explanations
  1. scoring matrix Turn 2. Scoring matrix records each proposal's assessment against the stated factors, allowing the totals and differences to be checked.
  2. weighted score Turn 4. Weighted score already incorporates the specified importance; applying the weights again would change the method and distort the totals.
  3. price premium Turn 6. Price premium is the ten-thousand-dollar difference between the two quoted prices, which the trade-off reasoning must address.
  4. evaluation narrative Turn 8. Evaluation narrative supplies the reasons and evidence behind scores rather than relying on a numerical total alone.
  5. evaluation factor Turn 10. Evaluation factor is a published assessment dimension; local address is not one of the stated criteria here.
  6. trade-off Turn 12. Trade-off compares the assessed benefits with the additional cost under the permitted method, retaining both favorable and unfavorable evidence.
  7. Best value Turn 14. Best value is the most advantageous result under the applicable selection method, not a universal consequence of a score or price.
  8. conflict of interest Turn 16. Conflict of interest concerns an interest that could influence official judgment; it requires the applicable disclosure and review process.
  9. award authority Turn 18. Award authority is the empowered decision-maker; the evaluation team's recommendation does not itself authorize or execute an award.
  10. audit trail Turn 20. Audit trail links the conclusion to the criteria, evidence, arithmetic, and approvals, allowing later review of the decision.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Marta: I can check the totals, but I cannot sign this rationale yet. Best value is doing a lot of work in the draft. Show me what sits behind it.
  2. Dev: The scoring matrix gives A forty-five for technical approach, twenty-five for service delivery, and sixteen for price. B has forty, twenty-four, and eighteen respectively.
  3. Marta: That gives eighty-six and eighty-two. Are those raw marks that still need the fifty, thirty, and twenty weighting applied?
  4. Dev: No, each weighted score is final for that factor. We should check the addition, but applying the weights again would change the evaluation rather than verify it.
  5. Marta: A is also ten thousand dollars more expensive. The note cannot simply report the higher total and leave that difference unexplained.
  6. Dev: Agreed. The price premium is part of the decision. A costs one hundred twenty thousand, while B costs one hundred ten thousand. Our method permits a documented trade-off.
  7. Marta: Which features support the technical difference? A reader should not have to infer a benefit from the score alone.
  8. Dev: I need to expand the evaluation narrative. Each assessed strength should point to the submitted material and the technical factor it addresses, rather than a general impression that A seems better.
  9. Marta: Someone suggested mentioning that A's office is local. Does the solicitation give us a factor for that?
  10. Dev: No. It is not an evaluation factor, so we should not add it now. The published dimensions are technical approach, service delivery, and price, with the stated relative importance.
  11. Marta: We also need to show that B's stronger price result was recognized, not quietly removed because the team prefers A.
  12. Dev: The trade-off explanation should do that. It must explain why the evidenced benefits of A are judged worth the premium, if that remains the supported recommendation.
  13. Marta: Is a four-point lead sufficient by itself? The summary says highest score therefore award, which skips the trade-off we have just discussed.
  14. Dev: No. Best value depends on this selection method and a defensible record. We should not present our scoring example as a rule for every procurement.
  15. Marta: Before circulation, check that any interests affecting an evaluator have gone through the proper disclosure process.
  16. Dev: I will confirm the conflict of interest review status. That belongs in the appropriate record, without turning an unsupported suspicion about a colleague into an allegation in the evaluation note.
  17. Marta: Once the explanation is complete, who actually authorizes the contract award? The draft sounds as though the evaluation team already has.
  18. Dev: The designated award authority still needs to decide. I will label the document as our recommendation and remove wording that suggests an award has already been made.
  19. Marta: Good. Keep the published criteria, the verified totals, the evidence, and the premium visible in the revised note.
  20. Dev: I will preserve that audit trail. The revision will explain the recommendation under the existing method, not introduce a new preference to make the result easier to defend.
Dialogue in the book, page 28 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

What does best value refer to?

The lesson's procurement evaluation note says best value without connecting that judgment to the published evaluation criteria.

  1. Procurement officer: The evaluation note says best value. Which published evaluation criteria support that judgment about the bidder?
  2. Evaluation panel member: The note doesn't identify them, so I need to revisit the evaluation record before explaining the conclusion.
  3. Procurement officer: Please clarify the evidence and criterion rather than add a general statement that the proposal feels stronger.
  4. Evaluation panel member: Would a comment about the bidder's reputation be enough to make the explanation more concrete?
  5. Procurement officer: Only the applicable evaluation framework can establish what belongs in the assessment. We shouldn't invent a criterion.
  6. Evaluation panel member: Then I should identify the relevant RFP requirement and the recorded evidence against it.
  7. Procurement officer: Yes. Here, RFP means request for proposals; its stated criteria matter to a traceable explanation.
  8. Evaluation panel member: I'll also ask for clarification if the record doesn't show how the judgment was reached.
  9. Procurement officer: Good. Keep the uncertainty visible rather than retrospectively presenting an unsupported rationale as established.
  10. Evaluation panel member: I'll return with the criterion-linked evidence or a precise question for the authorized procurement review.
An optional feature in the scoring discussion

Separate fictional case: a fictional RFP scores response time and implementation support; a panel member wants to award points for a dashboard feature not listed in those criteria.

  1. Panel chair: You mentioned awarding points for the dashboard. Which listed evaluation criterion would that assessment relate to?
  2. Technical evaluator: The RFP scores response time and implementation support. I don't see a separate dashboard criterion.
  3. Panel chair: Then we need procurement clarification before treating the feature as an additional scored advantage.
  4. Technical evaluator: Could it be evidence of implementation support rather than a new criterion in its own right?
  5. Panel chair: Possibly, but we should ask how the published framework applies rather than decide that informally here.
  6. Technical evaluator: I'll identify the feature and explain the proposed connection without changing the scoring sheet.
  7. Panel chair: Please also bring the exact proposal passage, so the question concerns documented evidence.
  8. Technical evaluator: Understood. Enthusiasm for a feature isn't enough to establish that it should affect the bidder's score.
  9. Panel chair: We'll seek the authorized interpretation and keep the current assessment status explicit while that question is open.
  10. Technical evaluator: I'll prepare the focused question and avoid adding a scoring rule during the discussion.
A quote with unclear installation scope

Separate fictional case: an evaluator compares two equipment bids; one clearly includes installation, while the other does not state whether installation is included.

  1. Procurement analyst: One bid includes installation. Could you confirm how installation is treated in the other bidder's quoted price?
  2. Technical reviewer: The second proposal doesn't say. I had assumed installation was included because the equipment is the same.
  3. Procurement analyst: Let's preserve that as unknown. The equipment description doesn't establish the scope of the quoted service.
  4. Technical reviewer: Should I contact the bidder directly and ask them to revise the price for installation?
  5. Procurement analyst: Use the authorized clarification process. We shouldn't improvise a contact or revision process in this discussion.
  6. Technical reviewer: Then I'll send procurement the exact ambiguity and the passages that need clarification.
  7. Procurement analyst: Good. We also need to distinguish clarifying the existing offer from requesting a changed offer.
  8. Technical reviewer: Until that distinction is resolved, I won't label the second bid cheaper on a like-for-like basis.
  9. Procurement analyst: Exactly. Our question should be specific enough for the responsible procurement team to handle appropriately.
  10. Technical reviewer: I'll mark installation scope unresolved and ask how it should be addressed under this procurement's rules.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

Best value needs a reason, not just a label
  1. Read turns 1-8, saying each factor score and total clearly.
  2. Swap roles for turns 9-20, stressing published factors and recommendation rather than award.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Explain another scored comparison

Under a stated trade-off method, C has already-weighted scores of 42, 26, and 17. D has 40, 25, and 19. C costs $75,000 and D $70,000. Award approval is pending.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Evaluator: "C's total weighted score is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. 85

Forty-two plus twenty-six plus seventeen equals eighty-five without applying weights again.

2. Officer: "D's total weighted score is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. 84

Forty plus twenty-five plus nineteen equals eighty-four under the supplied scoring record.

3. Evaluator: "C's price premium is ___."

Answer and explanation

D. $5,000

Seventy-five thousand minus seventy thousand gives a five-thousand-dollar premium.

4. Officer: "The award status is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. approval pending

The stated approval has not occurred, so a score comparison does not itself award the contract.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Lesson 03: Procurement and Vendor Selection
Original fictional case: A fictional office evaluates two service proposals using published maximum scores: technical approach 50, service delivery 30, and price 20. Recorded weighted scores are A: 45, 25, 16, total 86; B: 40, 24, 18, total 82. A costs $120,000 and B costs $110,000. The method permits a documented trade-off, but the draft note only says A is best value. Evaluator Dev and procurement officer Marta must explain the scored differences and the $10,000 premium. A vendor's local address is not a published criterion. The award has not been authorized.
Language workshop: Best value needs a reason, not just a label
Communication goal: Connect a procurement recommendation to published criteria, comparable evidence, and an authorized decision process.
Language guidance: The scores are already weighted. Do not apply the 50/30/20 maxima a second time. The totals are 86 and 82. A higher score is relevant under this method, but the permitted trade-off still needs a reasoned record of the benefits and premium.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): What does best value mean under this selection method? / Let us use the published evaluation factors. / The weighted scores total eighty-six for A and eighty-two for B. / These scores have already been weighted. / A carries a ten-thousand-dollar premium. / Which submitted feature supports the technical advantage? / Cite the proposal evidence beside the relevant criterion. / The note must explain why the assessed benefit justifies the premium. / Local address is not a published criterion. / Apply the same evidence standard to both offers. / This is an evaluation recommendation, not an award. / The designated award authority still needs to decide. / Any relevant interest should go through the conflict-review process. / The total does not replace the evaluation narrative. / A reader should be able to trace each conclusion to its evidence. / Revise the rationale without changing the published method.
Vocabulary: procurement: The process of obtaining goods or services under applicable rules. / solicitation: A formal request inviting offers under stated requirements. / offeror: A supplier submitting an offer in response to a solicitation. / evaluation factor: A stated dimension used to assess proposals. / subfactor: A defined component of a broader evaluation factor. / relative importance: The stated relationship in significance among evaluation factors. / weighted score: A score already adjusted to reflect the specified scoring importance. / scoring matrix: A structured record of scores against criteria. / technical approach: The proposed method for delivering the required work. / service-delivery plan: The proposed arrangement for providing the required service. / price premium: The amount by which one price exceeds another. / best value: The most advantageous result under the applicable selection method. / trade-off: A comparison accepting one disadvantage for a supported benefit. / strength: An assessed favorable feature under an evaluation criterion. / weakness: An assessed unfavorable feature under an evaluation criterion. / deficiency: A material failure to meet a stated requirement under the applicable method. / past performance: Evidence of how a supplier performed on relevant previous work. / evaluation narrative: Written reasoning supporting the recorded assessment. / source selection: The process of choosing a supplier under the applicable procedure. / award authority: The person or body empowered to authorize the contract award. / conflict of interest: An interest that may improperly influence, or appear to influence, official judgment. / recusal: Withdrawal from a matter under the applicable conflict-handling process. / audit trail: Records allowing a decision or transaction to be traced. / debriefing: A structured explanation of a procurement outcome under applicable procedures.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-8, saying each factor score and total clearly. Swap roles for turns 9-20, stressing published factors and recommendation rather than award. Complete the C-and-D comparison; verify the totals and premium before reading the result aloud.
Partner: Dev: Technical evaluator / Marta: Procurement officer
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Under a stated trade-off method, C has already-weighted scores of 42, 26, and 17. D has 40, 25, and 19. C costs $75,000 and D $70,000. Award approval is pending.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Marta: Once the explanation is complete, who actually authorizes the contract award? The draft sounds as though the evaluation team already has.
Dev: The designated award authority still needs to decide. I will label the document as our recommendation and remove wording that suggests an award has already been made.
Marta: Good. Keep the published criteria, the verified totals, the evidence, and the premium visible in the revised note.
Dev: I will preserve that audit trail. The revision will explain the recommendation under the existing method, not introduce a new preference to make the result easier to defend.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

04Grants, Compliance, and ReportingThe total matches the spreadsheet, not the evidence+

Your goal

Reconcile expenditure categories to supporting records and explain an unresolved difference without inventing a balancing entry.

This lesson in the book, page 31 ↗

A Read the situation

The total matches the spreadsheet, not the evidence

Grant G62 reports $48,000 spent in the quarter: personnel $30,000, materials $12,000, and travel $6,000. The reporting instructions request these categories and supporting records. The currently assembled records support personnel $29,000, materials $12,000, and travel $6,000, totaling $47,000. One $1,000 personnel item lacks the requested supporting record. Grant officer Sana and finance coordinator Owen must flag the evidence gap, seek the missing record, and follow the actual correction and submission process. The difference alone does not establish fraud, allowability, or authority to move the amount into another category.

Sana: Grant officer / Owen: Finance coordinator

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What total do the assembled supporting records substantiate?

Answer and explanation

B. $47,000

Twenty-nine thousand plus twelve thousand plus six thousand equals forty-seven thousand.

2. Where is the evidence gap?

Answer and explanation

A. A $1,000 personnel item

The personnel report exceeds the assembled personnel support by one thousand dollars.

3. What conclusion is not established by the difference alone?

Answer and explanation

B. Fraud or cost allowability

The discrepancy identifies missing support but does not determine intent or whether the cost is allowable.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

A reported total can add correctly while its supporting evidence remains incomplete. Here the category figures total $48,000, but the assembled support totals $47,000. The discrepancy is specifically a $1,000 personnel item, not a general arithmetic error.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
grant award
Funding provided under specified terms for an approved purpose.identify the grant award
recipient
The entity receiving an award directly from the awarding body.identify the recipient
subrecipient
An entity carrying out part of an award under a subaward.monitor the subrecipient
pass-through entity
An entity providing a subaward from funding it has received.contact the pass-through entity
award terms
The conditions governing a particular funding award.review the award terms
reporting period
The time interval covered by a report.confirm the reporting period
expenditure
An amount spent or recognized as spent under the applicable basis.report expenditures
budget category
A defined grouping of planned or reported costs.reconcile budget categories
personnel cost
Cost associated with staff work under the applicable accounting rules.document personnel costs
source documentation
Original or supporting records substantiating a transaction.obtain source documentation
supporting schedule
A detailed breakdown supporting a reported figure.prepare a supporting schedule
general ledger
The main accounting record organizing transactions by account.reconcile the general ledger
reconciliation
Comparison of records to identify and explain differences.complete the reconciliation
variance
A difference between compared figures or positions.explain the variance
unsupported item
An item lacking the evidence required for the stated review.flag an unsupported item
allowable cost
A cost permitted under the applicable rules and award terms.assess cost allowability
allocable cost
A cost assignable to an activity under the applicable benefit relationship.assess cost allocability
cost share
The portion of project costs met from sources specified outside the award funding.document cost share
indirect cost
A cost supporting multiple activities rather than one readily identified objective.review indirect costs
obligation
A commitment creating a requirement to pay under the applicable definition.distinguish obligations from expenditures
unobligated balance
Funds not committed through obligations under the applicable accounting basis.report the unobligated balance
certification
An authorized attestation about specified information or compliance.review the certification
corrected report
A revised submission fixing identified reporting errors.submit a corrected report
closeout
The process of completing award-end administrative and financial requirements.prepare for closeout

Documented does not automatically mean allowable, and unsupported does not automatically mean fraudulent. Obtain the required evidence and follow the actual review process. Do not move a cost between categories merely to make the report appear balanced.

C Notice the language

Use neutral discrepancy language

A missing record may lead to several possible explanations. State the amount, category, and evidence gap without accusing a colleague or making the problem disappear. A clear reconciliation lets the responsible reviewer decide what needs correction or further support.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Identify the award
We are reconciling the quarterly report for G62.
State the reported total
The report shows forty-eight thousand dollars.
State the supported total
The assembled records support forty-seven thousand dollars.
Locate the difference
The one-thousand-dollar gap is in personnel.
Separate arithmetic from evidence
The categories add correctly, but the supporting record is incomplete.
Request the missing record
Please locate the record for that specific personnel item.
Avoid an accusation
The gap does not by itself establish fraud.
Preserve the classification
Do not move the amount into travel just to balance the report.
Check the reporting basis
Are these figures for the same award, period, and accounting basis?
Qualify allowability
Supporting documentation does not alone settle cost allowability.
Keep the gap visible
Mark the item as unresolved in the reconciliation.
Ask about correction
What does the applicable process require before submission?
Avoid false certification
We should not certify completeness while this required support is missing.
Assign the follow-up
Owen will trace the item and return the evidence status.
Retain the record
Keep the original figures and any authorized correction traceable.
Close the review
The final report should match the approved treatment and supporting records.
6 language and precision notes
Support
Describes what the evidence substantiates, not merely what a spreadsheet states.
In personnel
Locates the discrepancy in a specific category.
Does not by itself
Limits a conclusion to what the evidence actually establishes.
Just to balance
Identifies an improper reason for changing classification.
Same award, period, and basis
Makes the reconciliation compare like with like.
Any authorized correction
Preserves the distinction between finding an issue and deciding its treatment.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which reconciliation statement is correct?

Answer and explanation

D. Reported expenditure is $48,000; assembled support is $47,000, with $1,000 unresolved in personnel.

The category sum is correct, but one personnel item lacks its requested supporting record.

2. What should Owen do first with the specific gap?

Answer and explanation

A. Trace the personnel item and seek the missing support.

Tracing the identified item addresses the actual evidence gap without inventing treatment or intent.

3. Which statement about allowability is sound?

Answer and explanation

B. It requires review under the applicable rules and award terms.

Evidence and correct arithmetic matter, but cost allowability also depends on the governing requirements.

4. What should a correction preserve?

Answer and explanation

C. A traceable record of the original figure, reason, approval, and revised treatment

A documented correction lets reviewers understand what changed, why, and under whose authority.

E Conversations

The total matches the spreadsheet, not the evidence

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Sana: Grant officer / Owen: Finance coordinator

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • variance
  • budget category
  • corrected report
  • reporting period
  • source documentation
  • certification
  • reconciliation
  • Allowable cost
  • supporting schedule
  • unsupported item
  1. Sana
    I cannot tie G62 to the evidence folder. The report says forty-eight thousand; I can support forty-seven. Can we work through the category totals together?
  2. Owen
    The 1 shows thirty thousand for personnel, twelve thousand for materials, and six thousand for travel. Those figures add correctly to the reported total.
  3. Sana
    The records I have show twenty-nine thousand for personnel. Materials and travel match the schedule. Is there another personnel record somewhere?
  4. Owen
    We are missing 2 for one thousand dollars in that category. I need to trace the specific item rather than assume it belongs in the folder.
  5. Sana
    Then the issue is not that the spreadsheet added the categories incorrectly. It is that the evidence does not yet support every reported item.
  6. Owen
    Correct. The 3 should show forty-eight thousand reported, forty-seven thousand supported, and one thousand unresolved in personnel. That is the clearest description of the current position.
  7. Sana
    Someone suggested moving the thousand into travel because that category has room in the budget. That would make the report look less awkward.
  8. Owen
    Available budget does not determine the 4 of an actual transaction. We should not reclassify the item simply to remove the visible gap. The treatment needs evidence and the proper review.
  9. Sana
    The draft issue note calls it fraud. All we know so far is that the record is missing. I want the wording to reflect that distinction.
  10. Owen
    Call it an 5 at this stage. Missing support does not by itself establish intent. We can state exactly what is absent and what we are doing to obtain it.
  11. Sana
    If you find the record, does that automatically settle whether the grant can pay for the cost?
  12. Owen
    No. 6 status depends on the applicable rules and award terms as well. The record supports the transaction; the reviewer still needs the relevant basis for charging it to the award.
  13. Sana
    Before comparing the figures again, please confirm that we are using the same quarter and the same accounting basis.
  14. Owen
    I will check the 7 and the award reference. We should not reconcile a current-quarter total against a folder containing a different period and then explain the wrong discrepancy.
  15. Sana
    The submission includes a completeness certification. I do not want us to sign it while ignoring a required missing record.
  16. Owen
    We should flag that before 8. I will return the evidence status and ask what the actual correction and submission process requires, rather than choosing a treatment on my own.
  17. Sana
    If a correction is authorized, keep the original report and the reason for the change. We need a clear history.
  18. Owen
    Agreed. A 9 should be traceable to the review decision. Quietly replacing the number would make it harder to understand what happened and whether the issue was actually resolved.
  19. Sana
    Please send the specific item reference, the missing evidence, and your follow-up status to me before we finalize the submission.
  20. Owen
    I will keep the 10 visible until it is resolved through that process. We can be precise about the gap without either hiding it or claiming more than the evidence shows.

Answers and explanations
  1. supporting schedule Turn 2. Supporting schedule breaks down the total into categories so the reported amount can be compared with the corresponding evidence.
  2. source documentation Turn 4. Source documentation substantiates the transaction; the requested record for one personnel item is absent from the assembled evidence.
  3. reconciliation Turn 6. Reconciliation compares the report with supporting records and identifies the precise difference rather than concealing it in the total.
  4. budget category Turn 8. Budget category should reflect the transaction's proper classification, not a convenient place to hide an unsupported amount.
  5. unsupported item Turn 10. Unsupported item accurately describes the missing required evidence without asserting fraudulent intent or a final cost decision.
  6. Allowable cost Turn 12. Allowable cost depends on the governing rules and award terms; finding a record alone does not decide that status.
  7. reporting period Turn 14. Reporting period defines the interval covered by the report; mismatched periods can create misleading differences in a reconciliation.
  8. certification Turn 16. Certification is an authorized attestation; an unresolved required evidence gap must not be hidden when completeness is attested.
  9. corrected report Turn 18. Corrected report records an authorized revision; the original amount, reason, and approval should remain traceable.
  10. variance Turn 20. Variance is the one-thousand-dollar difference between reported and supported figures; it remains visible until an evidenced treatment resolves it.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Sana: I cannot tie G62 to the evidence folder. The report says forty-eight thousand; I can support forty-seven. Can we work through the category totals together?
  2. Owen: The supporting schedule shows thirty thousand for personnel, twelve thousand for materials, and six thousand for travel. Those figures add correctly to the reported total.
  3. Sana: The records I have show twenty-nine thousand for personnel. Materials and travel match the schedule. Is there another personnel record somewhere?
  4. Owen: We are missing source documentation for one thousand dollars in that category. I need to trace the specific item rather than assume it belongs in the folder.
  5. Sana: Then the issue is not that the spreadsheet added the categories incorrectly. It is that the evidence does not yet support every reported item.
  6. Owen: Correct. The reconciliation should show forty-eight thousand reported, forty-seven thousand supported, and one thousand unresolved in personnel. That is the clearest description of the current position.
  7. Sana: Someone suggested moving the thousand into travel because that category has room in the budget. That would make the report look less awkward.
  8. Owen: Available budget does not determine the budget category of an actual transaction. We should not reclassify the item simply to remove the visible gap. The treatment needs evidence and the proper review.
  9. Sana: The draft issue note calls it fraud. All we know so far is that the record is missing. I want the wording to reflect that distinction.
  10. Owen: Call it an unsupported item at this stage. Missing support does not by itself establish intent. We can state exactly what is absent and what we are doing to obtain it.
  11. Sana: If you find the record, does that automatically settle whether the grant can pay for the cost?
  12. Owen: No. Allowable cost status depends on the applicable rules and award terms as well. The record supports the transaction; the reviewer still needs the relevant basis for charging it to the award.

  14. Owen: I will check the reporting period and the award reference. We should not reconcile a current-quarter total against a folder containing a different period and then explain the wrong discrepancy.

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

The total needs supporting categories

The lesson's grant report contains total expenditure but omits the supporting categories requested for reporting.

  1. Grants officer: Could you provide the expenditure breakdown in the requested categories so we can review this grant report?
  2. Program accountant: The total is already included. Do you need the categories even if the overall amount is unchanged?
  3. Grants officer: Yes. The total doesn't show how expenditure relates to the reporting categories or supporting records.
  4. Program accountant: Should I divide the total evenly between the categories to produce a complete-looking report?
  5. Grants officer: No. Please use the actual records and identify anything that cannot yet be allocated accurately.
  6. Program accountant: Understood. I'll check the source entries and ask the relevant owners about any unclear classifications.
  7. Grants officer: Please also flag items needing allowable-cost review rather than treating inclusion in a total as approval.
  8. Program accountant: I can explain what information is available and estimate the remaining preparation time after checking the records.
  9. Grants officer: That would help. Let me know the realistic timing instead of assuming a new reporting deadline.
  10. Program accountant: I'll return with the supported breakdown and a separate list of unresolved entries for review.
A subrecipient's missing reporting period

Separate fictional case: a subrecipient submits a cost schedule labeled quarter total without dates; the grant team needs the April-through-June reporting period.

  1. Grant reporting coordinator: Could you confirm the dates covered by your quarter total? Our request concerns April through June.
  2. Subrecipient finance officer: The schedule label is incomplete. I'll check the underlying records rather than assume it covers those months.
  3. Grant reporting coordinator: Please send the date range with the supporting schedule so we can match it to the reporting period.
  4. Subrecipient finance officer: If the schedule includes another month, would you like me to adjust the total from memory?
  5. Grant reporting coordinator: No. Reconcile it to the dated records and identify any adjustment clearly.
  6. Subrecipient finance officer: Understood. The request is for a supported period-specific schedule, not just a renamed attachment.
  7. Grant reporting coordinator: Exactly. Could you tell me when you can confirm the period after checking the source entries?
  8. Subrecipient finance officer: I'll check access to those entries today and then give you a realistic response time.
  9. Grant reporting coordinator: Thank you. We'll keep the period match open until the dates and amounts are verified.
  10. Subrecipient finance officer: I'll send the confirmed range, supporting detail, and any remaining question through our reporting channel.
An invoice without the grant reference

Separate fictional case: a fictional grant report includes an invoice for shared training, but no allocation record links the charge to this grant.

  1. Grant reviewer: Please provide the allocation record for this shared training invoice so we can review the amount charged here.
  2. Program finance lead: The training benefited several projects. Is the invoice alone insufficient to explain this grant's share?
  3. Grant reviewer: It shows a supplier charge, but it doesn't establish how the grant allocation was calculated.
  4. Program finance lead: I'll ask the budget owner for the basis and supporting record used for the allocation.
  5. Grant reviewer: Thank you. Keep any allowable-cost question separate; I haven't approved the charge by requesting more evidence.
  6. Program finance lead: Could we submit the current report while labeling that item as reviewed to avoid another delay?
  7. Grant reviewer: Initial review identified missing allocation evidence; we must not describe the charge as approved or the review as complete.
  8. Program finance lead: Understood. I'll report the gap explicitly and request the applicable submission instruction.
  9. Grant reviewer: Please confirm when the allocation evidence can be supplied and whether any source record is unavailable.
  10. Program finance lead: I'll return with those details, without inventing a split or implying the expense is already accepted.

Answer and explanation

Answer and explanation

C. $27,500

Eighteen thousand plus six thousand five hundred plus three thousand equals twenty-seven thousand five hundred.

Answer and explanation

D. $500

The reported total exceeds the assembled support by five hundred dollars.

Answer and explanation

C. materials

Personnel and travel match their support; the five-hundred-dollar difference is in materials.

Lesson 04: Grants, Compliance, and Reporting
Original fictional case: Grant G62 reports $48,000 spent in the quarter: personnel $30,000, materials $12,000, and travel $6,000. The reporting instructions request these categories and supporting records. The currently assembled records support personnel $29,000, materials $12,000, and travel $6,000, totaling $47,000. One $1,000 personnel item lacks the requested supporting record. Grant officer Sana and finance coordinator Owen must flag the evidence gap, seek the missing record, and follow the actual correction and submission process. The difference alone does not establish fraud, allowability, or authority to move the amount into another category.
Language workshop: The total matches the spreadsheet, not the evidence
Communication goal: Reconcile expenditure categories to supporting records and explain an unresolved difference without inventing a balancing entry.
Language guidance: A reported total can add correctly while its supporting evidence remains incomplete. Here the category figures total $48,000, but the assembled support totals $47,000. The discrepancy is specifically a $1,000 personnel item, not a general arithmetic error.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): We are reconciling the quarterly report for G62. / The report shows forty-eight thousand dollars. / The assembled records support forty-seven thousand dollars. / The one-thousand-dollar gap is in personnel. / The categories add correctly, but the supporting record is incomplete. / Please locate the record for that specific personnel item. / The gap does not by itself establish fraud. / Do not move the amount into travel just to balance the report. / Are these figures for the same award, period, and accounting basis? / Supporting documentation does not alone settle cost allowability. / Mark the item as unresolved in the reconciliation. / What does the applicable process require before submission? / We should not certify completeness while this required support is missing. / Owen will trace the item and return the evidence status. / Keep the original figures and any authorized correction traceable. / The final report should match the approved treatment and supporting records.
Vocabulary: grant award: Funding provided under specified terms for an approved purpose. / recipient: The entity receiving an award directly from the awarding body. / subrecipient: An entity carrying out part of an award under a subaward. / pass-through entity: An entity providing a subaward from funding it has received. / award terms: The conditions governing a particular funding award. / reporting period: The time interval covered by a report. / expenditure: An amount spent or recognized as spent under the applicable basis. / budget category: A defined grouping of planned or reported costs. / personnel cost: Cost associated with staff work under the applicable accounting rules. / source documentation: Original or supporting records substantiating a transaction. / supporting schedule: A detailed breakdown supporting a reported figure. / general ledger: The main accounting record organizing transactions by account. / reconciliation: Comparison of records to identify and explain differences. / variance: A difference between compared figures or positions. / unsupported item: An item lacking the evidence required for the stated review. / allowable cost: A cost permitted under the applicable rules and award terms. / allocable cost: A cost assignable to an activity under the applicable benefit relationship. / cost share: The portion of project costs met from sources specified outside the award funding. / indirect cost: A cost supporting multiple activities rather than one readily identified objective. / obligation: A commitment creating a requirement to pay under the applicable definition. / unobligated balance: Funds not committed through obligations under the applicable accounting basis. / certification: An authorized attestation about specified information or compliance. / corrected report: A revised submission fixing identified reporting errors. / closeout: The process of completing award-end administrative and financial requirements.

Vocabulary: grant award: Funding provided under specified terms for an approved purpose. / recipient: The entity receiving an award directly from the awarding body. / subrecipient: An entity carrying out part of an award under a subaward. / pass-through entity: An entity providing a subaward from funding it has received. / award terms: The conditions governing a particular funding award. / reporting period: The time interval covered by a report. / expenditure: An amount spent or recognized as spent under the applicable basis. / budget category: A defined grouping of planned or reported costs. / personnel cost: Cost associated with staff work under the applicable accounting rules. / source documentation: Original or supporting records substantiating a transaction. / supporting schedule: A detailed breakdown supporting a reported figure. / general ledger: The main accounting record organizing transactions by account. / reconciliation: Comparison of records to identify and explain differences. / variance: A difference between compared figures or positions. / unsupported item: An item lacking the evidence required for the stated review. / allowable cost: A cost permitted under the applicable rules and award terms. / allocable cost: A cost assignable to an activity under the applicable benefit relationship. / cost share: The portion of project costs met from sources specified outside the award funding. / indirect cost: A cost supporting multiple activities rather than one readily identified objective. / obligation: A commitment creating a requirement to pay under the applicable definition. / unobligated balance: Funds not committed through obligations under the applicable accounting basis. / certification: An authorized attestation about specified information or compliance. / corrected report: A revised submission fixing identified reporting errors. / closeout: The process of completing award-end administrative and financial requirements.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-8, distinguishing the reported amount from the supported amount. Swap roles for turns 9-20; keep the unresolved item neutral and specific. Complete the K9 reconciliation and check that its difference belongs to materials, not personnel.
Partner: Sana: Grant officer / Owen: Finance coordinator
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Grant K9 reports personnel $18,000, materials $7,000, and travel $3,000. Assembled support is $18,000, $6,500, and $3,000 respectively. One materials record remains missing.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Sana: If a correction is authorized, keep the original report and the reason for the change. We need a clear history.
Owen: Agreed. A corrected report should be traceable to the review decision. Quietly replacing the number would make it harder to understand what happened and whether the issue was actually resolved.
Sana: Please send the specific item reference, the missing evidence, and your follow-up status to me before we finalize the submission.
Owen: I will keep the variance visible until it is resolved through that process. We can be precise about the gap without either hiding it or claiming more than the evidence shows.
END REFERENCE
Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

Course: Government and Public Administration English
Professional audience: public administrators, agency program managers, policy analysts, procurement staff, constituent-service teams, grants managers, and interagency coordinators
Scope: All programs, residents, offices, applications, figures, meetings, and local procedures are fictional. This is professional-English instruction, not legal advice or authority to decide eligibility, award contracts, release records, or spend funds. U.S. federal references support selected terminology; their rules do not automatically govern every local, state, tribal, or international office.
Lesson 04: Grants, Compliance, and Reporting
Original fictional case: Grant G62 reports $48,000 spent in the quarter: personnel $30,000, materials $12,000, and travel $6,000. The reporting instructions request these categories and supporting records. The currently assembled records support personnel $29,000, materials $12,000, and travel $6,000, totaling $47,000. One $1,000 personnel item lacks the requested supporting record. Grant officer Sana and finance coordinator Owen must flag the evidence gap, seek the missing record, and follow the actual correction and submission process. The difference alone does not establish fraud, allowability, or authority to move the amount into another category.
Language workshop: The total matches the spreadsheet, not the evidence
Communication goal: Reconcile expenditure categories to supporting records and explain an unresolved difference without inventing a balancing entry.

05Interagency CoordinationEveryone is involved, but nobody has called the resident+

Your goal

Assign distinct responsibilities across agencies and confirm a handoff without implying that the underlying service is approved.

This lesson in the book, page 40 ↗

A Read the situation

Everyone is involved, but nobody has called the resident

Request Q41 concerns accessible transport to a community appointment. The Community Office reviews program eligibility; the Transit Office checks vehicle availability and booking feasibility. Each office assumed the other would contact the resident. During a coordination call, Jon from Community agrees to own the resident update at 15:00 local time. Aisha from Transit will send an availability status to Jon by 14:30. Eligibility and a booking both remain unconfirmed. The authorized initial handoff needs the request reference, pickup zone, and requested time window, not the resident's full medical file.

Jon: Community Office coordinator / Aisha: Transit Office coordinator

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. Who owns the resident update?

Answer and explanation

B. Jon at 15:00 local time

Jon explicitly accepts responsibility for the resident update at the stated local time.

2. What will Transit provide by 14:30?

Answer and explanation

D. An availability status to Jon

Aisha commits to sending a status on availability, not approving eligibility or guaranteeing a booking.

3. What remains unconfirmed?

Answer and explanation

A. Eligibility and the transport booking

The offices have assigned communication tasks, but neither the eligibility decision nor booking is confirmed.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Receipt means a message arrived. Acceptance of responsibility means someone has agreed to perform the next task. Neither proves that the resident is eligible or that a vehicle is booked. Record these different statuses separately in the shared request record.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
interagency
Involving two or more public agencies.coordinate interagency work
lead contact
The named person coordinating communication for a matter.appoint a lead contact
referring office
The office sending a matter to another responsible team.identify the referring office
receiving office
The office to which a matter is transferred.confirm the receiving office
responsibility matrix
A record mapping tasks to their responsible owners.update the responsibility matrix
action owner
The named person accountable for a specific next action.assign an action owner
handoff
Transfer of a task, information, or responsibility between teams.confirm the handoff
acknowledgment of receipt
Confirmation that information has arrived.request acknowledgment of receipt
acceptance of responsibility
Confirmation that a person or team has taken ownership of a task.record acceptance of responsibility
service dependency
A prerequisite affecting whether a service can proceed.identify service dependencies
availability check
A review of whether the needed resource or slot can be supplied.complete an availability check
booking confirmation
Notice that a specific service arrangement is reserved or agreed.issue booking confirmation
eligibility review
Assessment against the program's qualification requirements.complete the eligibility review
request reference
An identifier linking records to the same service request.quote the request reference
pickup zone
The defined area from which a transport pickup is requested.confirm the pickup zone
requested window
The time period asked for before scheduling is confirmed.record the requested window
data-sharing authority
The applicable basis permitting a defined information transfer.verify data-sharing authority
minimum necessary data
Information limited to what is needed for the stated authorized purpose.share the minimum necessary data
purpose limitation
Restricting information use to the defined permitted purpose.observe purpose limitation
secure channel
An approved communication route with appropriate safeguards.use the secure channel
joint status
A coordinated account of progress across participating teams.prepare a joint status
escalation contact
The person designated for unresolved or urgent coordination issues.identify the escalation contact
dependency tracker
A record of prerequisites, owners, and their status.maintain the dependency tracker
closed-loop handoff
A transfer in which receipt and next responsibility are confirmed.complete a closed-loop handoff

Share information under the applicable authority and safeguards, limited to the task. Here the authorized initial exchange needs a reference, pickup zone, and requested window. Do not attach a full medical file merely because both teams are public agencies.

C Notice the language

Shared work still needs named actions

Saying we are handling it can hide a gap between organizations. Assign one person to each action and repeat the recipient, deadline, and expected result. Keep responsibility for communicating progress distinct from authority to approve eligibility or confirm a booking.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Name the gap
Each office assumed the other would contact the resident.
Assign the lead
Jon will own the resident update.
Set the external time
The resident update is at 15:00 local time.
Set the internal time
Transit will send its status to Jon by 14:30.
Define the task
We are checking availability, not confirming a booking yet.
Keep the eligibility boundary
Community retains the eligibility review.
Separate receipt from ownership
Has the receiving office accepted the task, or only received the message?
Use the reference
Please quote Q41 in both records.
Limit the transfer
The initial handoff needs the pickup zone and requested window.
Avoid excess information
The full medical file is not needed for this exchange.
Check permission and method
Use the authorized data-sharing route and approved channel.
Confirm the recipient
Send the availability status to Jon, not directly to an unassigned inbox.
Handle an incomplete result
Report the open question even if availability is not confirmed by then.
Name escalation
Who should we contact if the internal update is missed?
Read back ownership
Aisha checks availability; Jon updates the resident.
Close the loop
Please confirm receipt and the next action in the request record.
6 language and precision notes
Own the update
Assigns communication accountability without granting decision authority.
By 14:30
Gives the internal contribution a deadline before the resident contact.
Not ... yet
Prevents an investigation task from being mistaken for a completed result.
Retains
Keeps an existing responsibility with the correct office.
Only received
Distinguishes message delivery from task ownership.
Even if
Preserves the update commitment when the underlying answer remains incomplete.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which handoff is complete?

Answer and explanation

C. Aisha sends the availability status by 14:30; Jon confirms receipt and updates the resident at 15:00.

The complete handoff names actions, recipients, timing, and confirmation without claiming service approval.

2. Which statement preserves the agencies' roles?

Answer and explanation

D. Community reviews eligibility; Transit checks availability.

The brief assigns these distinct responsibilities and leaves both substantive outcomes open.

3. Which information belongs in the authorized initial exchange?

Answer and explanation

B. Request reference, pickup zone, and requested window

The defined initial task needs the specified limited information, not unnecessary sensitive records.

4. What should happen if availability remains unknown at 14:30?

Answer and explanation

D. Aisha sends that status and the open question so Jon can give the promised update.

A progress update remains useful and due even when the underlying resource decision is incomplete.

E Conversations

Everyone is involved, but nobody has called the resident

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Jon: Community Office coordinator / Aisha: Transit Office coordinator

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

    We have a gap on Q41. The resident has heard from neither office. Our note says Transit to contact, but I cannot find an accepted handoff.
  2. Aisha
    We thought Community was the 1. Let us assign that explicitly now rather than leave another shared note that neither office owns.
  3. Jon
    I will update the resident at 15:00 local time. I need your availability position early enough to explain what is still open.
  4. Aisha
    I will be the 2 for the Transit status and send it to you by 14:30. That gives you a defined input before your contact.
  5. Jon
    Is that a booking confirmation, or simply the result of the availability check at that point?
  6. Aisha
    It is an 3 status. We have not confirmed a vehicle or a booking. I will distinguish available, unavailable, and still under review rather than use the vague word arranged.
  7. Jon
    Community still needs to complete its program review. I do not want either team to imply that the transport request itself establishes eligibility.
  8. Aisha
    Keep the 4 with Community. Transit can report its resource position, but that does not replace your office's decision or remove the other service dependencies.
  9. Jon
    What do you need in the initial handoff? The file contains personal details that may not be relevant to checking a possible slot.
  10. Aisha
    Under the authorized exchange, send the 5, pickup zone, and requested time window. We do not need the full medical file for that initial task.
  11. Jon
    I will use the approved channel and keep the information limited to that purpose. Should I send it to your personal work queue?
  12. Aisha
    Yes, through the designated 6. I will confirm that it arrived and that I have accepted the availability task, not just that the message is visible.
  13. Jon
    That is where we lost the thread last time. Sent appeared in the record, and everyone read it as taken over. I will separate those statuses.
  14. Aisha
    An 7 alone would not show ownership. I will record the next action and its deadline so your team can see what I have actually agreed to do.
  15. Jon
    If you still cannot confirm availability at 14:30, please tell me that rather than wait until you have a final answer.
  16. Aisha
    Agreed. The 8 can explain the open questions. You should be able to give the resident the promised update even if the booking decision is still pending.
  17. Jon
    We also need a route if the internal update is missed or a dependency cannot be resolved within the available time.
  18. Aisha
    I will identify the 9 in the record. That is a coordination route, not a promise to override eligibility requirements or create capacity that does not exist.
  19. Jon

  20. Aisha
    Correct. That is a 10. I will confirm the transfer in Q41 and keep my status separate from any booking confirmation so both offices communicate the same position.

Answers and explanations
  1. lead contact Turn 2. Lead contact identifies who coordinates resident communication, resolving the earlier ambiguity between offices.
  2. action owner Turn 4. Action owner assigns Aisha the Transit status task, distinct from Jon's resident update.
  3. availability check Turn 6. Availability check assesses resources; it does not automatically confirm a reservation or a completed journey.
  4. eligibility review Turn 8. Eligibility review remains Community's task; coordination assignments do not decide whether the resident qualifies.
  5. request reference Turn 10. Request reference links the offices' records to Q41 without repeating unnecessary personal information.
  6. secure channel Turn 12. Secure channel is the approved route for this authorized exchange, preserving safeguards while the two offices coordinate.
  7. acknowledgment of receipt Turn 14. Acknowledgment of receipt confirms arrival, not task ownership; acceptance must also be explicit.
  8. joint status Turn 16. Joint status combines both offices' progress and open questions without implying completed decisions.
  9. escalation contact Turn 18. Escalation contact provides a named route for unresolved coordination issues, preventing another round of unowned messages.
  10. closed-loop handoff Turn 20. Closed-loop handoff confirms receipt, ownership, and next actions instead of relying on message delivery alone.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Jon: We have a gap on Q41. The resident has heard from neither office. Our note says Transit to contact, but I cannot find an accepted handoff.
  2. Aisha: We thought Community was the lead contact. Let us assign that explicitly now rather than leave another shared note that neither office owns.

  7. Jon: Community still needs to complete its program review. I do not want either team to imply that the transport request itself establishes eligibility.
  8. Aisha: Keep the eligibility review with Community. Transit can report its resource position, but that does not replace your office's decision or remove the other service dependencies.
  9. Jon: What do you need in the initial handoff? The file contains personal details that may not be relevant to checking a possible slot.
  10. Aisha: Under the authorized exchange, send the request reference, pickup zone, and requested time window. We do not need the full medical file for that initial task.
  11. Jon: I will use the approved channel and keep the information limited to that purpose. Should I send it to your personal work queue?
  12. Aisha: Yes, through the designated secure channel. I will confirm that it arrived and that I have accepted the availability task, not just that the message is visible.
  13. Jon: That is where we lost the thread last time. Sent appeared in the record, and everyone read it as taken over. I will separate those statuses.
